```
     1→class TerminalManager
     2→  DATA_DIR = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→  WETTY_IMAGE = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_WETTY_IMAGE", "wettyoss/wetty:latest")
     4→  NETWORK_NAME = "sandcastle-web"
     5→  DYNAMIC_DIR = File.join(DATA_DIR, "traefik", "dynamic")
     6→
     7→  class Error < StandardError; end
     8→
     9→  # Opens a web terminal for the given sandbox.
    10→  # Returns the URL path to the WeTTY session.
    11→  def open(sandbox:)
    12→    raise Error, "Sandbox is not running" unless sandbox.status == "running"
    13→    raise Error, "Sandbox has no container" if sandbox.container_id.blank?
    14→
    15→    user = sandbox.user
    16→    container_name = wetty_container_name(sandbox)
    17→
    18→    # Idempotent: if WeTTY container already running, ensure config exists and return
    19→    if container_running?(container_name)
    20→      write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    21→      return wetty_url(sandbox)
    22→    end
    23→
    24→    pull_image
    25→    ensure_network
    26→    connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)
    27→
    28→    # Write Traefik config early so it has time to detect the new route
    29→    # while we set up keypairs and start the WeTTY container.
    30→    write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    31→
    32→    key_dir = generate_keypair(sandbox)
    33→    inject_pubkey(sandbox, key_dir)
    34→    create_wetty_container(sandbox: sandbox, user: user, key_dir: key_dir)
    35→
    36→    wetty_url(sandbox)
    37→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    38→    raise Error, "Failed to open terminal: #{e.message}"
    39→  rescue SystemCallError => e
    40→    raise Error, "Failed to open terminal: #{e.message}"
    41→  end
    42→
    43→  # Closes the web terminal for the given sandbox.
    44→  def close(sandbox:)
    45→    container_name = wetty_container_name(sandbox)
    46→
    47→    # Stop and remove WeTTY container
    48→    begin
    49→      container = Docker::Container.get(container_name)
    50→      container.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
    51→      container.delete(force: true)
    52→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
    53→      # Already gone
    54→    end
    55→
    56→    # Delete Traefik config
    57→    delete_traefik_config(sandbox)
    58→
    59→    # Clean up key directory
    60→    key_dir = key_dir_path(sandbox)
    61→    FileUtils.rm_rf(key_dir)
    62→
    63→    # Best-effort: remove pubkey from sandbox authorized_keys
    64→    remove_pubkey(sandbox)
    65→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    66→    Rails.logger.error("TerminalManager: close failed for #{sandbox.full_name}: #{e.message}")
    67→  end
    68→
    69→  # Returns true if the WeTTY container is running AND its HTTP server is ready.
    70→  def active?(sandbox:)
    71→    container_name = wetty_container_name(sandbox)
    72→    return false unless container_running?(container_name)
    73→
    74→    TCPSocket.new(container_name, 3000).close
    75→    true
    76→  rescue Errno::ECONNREFUSED, Errno::ETIMEDOUT, SocketError
    77→    false
    78→  end
    79→
    80→  # Removes orphaned WeTTY containers whose sandbox no longer exists or is not running.
    81→  def cleanup_orphaned
    82→    Docker::Container.all(all: true).each do |container|
    83→      name = container.info.dig("Names")&.first&.delete_prefix("/")
    84→      next unless name&.start_with?("sc-wetty-")
    85→
    86→      labels = container.info["Labels"] || {}
    87→      sandbox_id = labels["sandcastle.sandbox_id"]&.to_i
    88→
    89→      sandbox = sandbox_id ? Sandbox.find_by(id: sandbox_id) : nil
    90→      should_remove = sandbox.nil? || sandbox.status != "running"
    91→
    92→      if should_remove
    93→        container.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
    94→        container.delete(force: true)
    95→        Rails.logger.info("TerminalManager: removed orphaned WeTTY container #{name}")
    96→
    97→        # Clean up Traefik config and keys if we have a sandbox_id
    98→        if sandbox_id
    99→          config_path = File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "terminal-#{sandbox_id}.yml")
   100→          File.delete(config_path) if File.exist?(config_path)
   101→        end
   102→
   103→        # Extract full_name from container name and validate format
   104→        full_name = name.delete_prefix("sc-wetty-")
   105→        unless full_name.match?(/\A[a-z][a-z0-9_-]+-[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*\z/)
   106→          Rails.logger.warn("TerminalManager: skipping suspicious container name: #{name}")
   107→          next
   108→        end
   109→
   110→        key_dir = File.join(DATA_DIR, "wetty", full_name)
   111→        expected_parent = File.join(DATA_DIR, "wetty")
   112→        unless File.expand_path(key_dir).start_with?("#{File.expand_path(expected_parent)}/")
   113→          Rails.logger.warn("TerminalManager: path traversal attempt detected: #{key_dir}")
   114→          next
   115→        end
   116→
   117→        FileUtils.rm_rf(key_dir) if Dir.exist?(key_dir)
   118→      end
   119→    end
   120→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   121→    Rails.logger.error("TerminalManager: orphan cleanup failed: #{e.message}")
   122→  end
   123→
   124→  private
   125→
   126→  def wetty_container_name(sandbox)
   127→    "sc-wetty-#{sandbox.full_name}"
   128→  end
   129→
   130→  def wetty_url(sandbox)
   131→    "/terminal/#{sandbox.id}/wetty"
   132→  end
   133→
   134→  def key_dir_path(sandbox)
   135→    File.join(DATA_DIR, "wetty", sandbox.full_name)
   136→  end
   137→
   138→  def container_running?(name)
   139→    container = Docker::Container.get(name)
   140→    container.json.dig("State", "Running") == true
   141→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   142→    false
   143→  end
   144→
   145→  def pull_image
   146→    Docker::Image.get(WETTY_IMAGE)
   147→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   148→    Docker::Image.create("fromImage" => WETTY_IMAGE)
   149→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   150→    raise Error, "Failed to pull #{WETTY_IMAGE} — check network connectivity"
   151→  end
   152→
   153→  def ensure_network
   154→    Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   155→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   156→    Docker::Network.create(NETWORK_NAME, "Driver" => "bridge")
   157→  end
   158→
   159→  def connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)
   160→    return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   161→
   162→    network = Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   163→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   164→
   165→    networks = container.json.dig("NetworkSettings", "Networks") || {}
   166→    return if networks.key?(NETWORK_NAME)
   167→
   168→    network.connect(sandbox.container_id)
   169→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   170→    # Container no longer exists - sync job will fix the DB state
   171→    raise Error, "Sandbox container not found. Please refresh and try again."
   172→  end
   173→
   174→  def generate_keypair(sandbox)
   175→    key_dir = key_dir_path(sandbox)
   176→    # Remove entire dir to avoid stale keys with bad permissions from prior sessions
   177→    FileUtils.rm_rf(key_dir)
   178→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(key_dir, mode: 0o700)
   179→
   180→    key_path = File.join(key_dir, "key")
   181→
   182→    system("ssh-keygen", "-t", "ed25519", "-f", key_path, "-N", "", "-q", "-C", "wetty-#{sandbox.full_name}",
   183→      exception: true)
   184→
   185→    # Ensure private key is only readable by owner
   186→    File.chmod(0o600, key_path)
   187→
   188→    key_dir
   189→  end
   190→
   191→  def inject_pubkey(sandbox, key_dir)
   192→    pubkey = File.read(File.join(key_dir, "key.pub")).strip
   193→    username = sandbox.user.name
   194→
   195→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   196→
   197→    # Write pubkey via base64 to avoid any shell-injection risk.
   198→    # The stdin: approach hangs because docker-api never sends EOF to cat.
   199→    encoded = Base64.strict_encode64("#{pubkey}\n")
   200→    container.exec([ "mkdir", "-p", "/home/#{username}/.ssh" ])
   201→    container.exec([ "sh", "-c", "echo #{encoded} | base64 -d >> /home/#{username}/.ssh/authorized_keys" ])
   202→    container.exec([ "chown", "-R", "#{username}:#{username}", "/home/#{username}/.ssh" ])
   203→    container.exec([ "chmod", "600", "/home/#{username}/.ssh/authorized_keys" ])
   204→  end
   205→
   206→  def create_wetty_container(sandbox:, user:, key_dir:)
   207→    container_name = wetty_container_name(sandbox)
   208→
   209→    # Remove any existing container with this name
   210→    begin
   211→      old = Docker::Container.get(container_name)
   212→      old.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
   213→      old.delete(force: true)
   214→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   215→      # No existing container
   216→    end
   217→
   218→    ssh_command = [
   219→      "ssh", "-p", "22",
   220→      "-o", "StrictHostKeyChecking=no",
   221→      "-o", "UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null",
   222→      "-o", "LogLevel=ERROR",
   223→      "-i", "/etc/wetty/key",
   224→      "#{user.name}@#{sandbox.full_name}",
   225→      "-t", "tmux new-session -A -s main"
   226→    ].join(" ")
   227→
   228→    container = Docker::Container.create(
   229→      "name" => container_name,
   230→      "Image" => WETTY_IMAGE,
   231→      "Env" => [
   232→        "COMMAND=#{ssh_command}",
   233→        "BASE=/terminal/#{sandbox.id}/wetty"
   234→      ],
   235→      "HostConfig" => {
   236→        "NetworkMode" => NETWORK_NAME,
   237→        "RestartPolicy" => { "Name" => "no" },
   238→        "Memory" => 128 * 1024 * 1024, # 128MB
   239→        "NanoCpus" => 500_000_000 # 0.5 CPU
   240→      },
   241→      "Labels" => {
   242→        "sandcastle.sandbox_id" => sandbox.id.to_s,
   243→        "sandcastle.role" => "wetty"
   244→      }
   245→    )
   246→
   247→    container.start
   248→    copy_key_to_container(container, key_dir)
   249→    container
   250→  end
   251→
   252→  # Copy the SSH private key into the WeTTY container via exec + base64.
   253→  # This avoids bind-mount path issues when Rails runs inside a container
   254→  # with a Docker volume for /data (the host path doesn't match).
   255→  def copy_key_to_container(container, key_dir)
   256→    key_content = File.read(File.join(key_dir, "key"))
   257→    encoded = Base64.strict_encode64(key_content)
   258→
   259→    container.exec([ "mkdir", "-p", "/etc/wetty" ])
   260→    container.exec([ "sh", "-c", "echo #{encoded} | base64 -d > /etc/wetty/key" ])
   261→    container.exec([ "chmod", "600", "/etc/wetty/key" ])
   262→  end
   263→
   264→  def write_traefik_config(sandbox)
   265→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
   266→
   267→    host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "localhost")
   268→    id = sandbox.id
   269→    container_name = wetty_container_name(sandbox)
   270→
   271→    rule = if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   272→      "HostRegexp(`.+`) && PathPrefix(`/terminal/#{id}/wetty`)"
   273→    else
   274→      "Host(`#{host}`) && PathPrefix(`/terminal/#{id}/wetty`)"
   275→    end
   276→
   277→    config = {
   278→      "http" => {
   279→        "routers" => {
   280→          "terminal-#{id}" => {
   281→            "rule" => rule,
   282→            "service" => "terminal-#{id}",
   283→            "entryPoints" => [ "websecure" ],
   284→            "tls" => tls_config,
   285→            "middlewares" => [ "terminal-auth-#{id}" ],
   286→            "priority" => 100
   287→          }
   288→        },
   289→        "middlewares" => {
   290→          "terminal-auth-#{id}" => {
   291→            "forwardAuth" => {
   292→              "address" => "http://sandcastle-web:80/terminal/auth",
   293→              "trustForwardHeader" => true
   294→            }
   295→          }
   296→        },
   297→        "services" => {
   298→          "terminal-#{id}" => {
   299→            "loadBalancer" => {
   300→              "servers" => [ { "url" => "http://#{container_name}:3000" } ]
   301→            }
   302→          }
   303→        }
   304→      }
   305→    }
   306→
   307→    File.write(File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "terminal-#{id}.yml"), config.to_yaml)
   308→  end
   309→
   310→  def tls_config
   311→    if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   312→      {}
   313→    else
   314→      { "certResolver" => "letsencrypt" }
   315→    end
   316→  end
   317→
   318→  def delete_traefik_config(sandbox)
   319→    path = File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "terminal-#{sandbox.id}.yml")
   320→    File.delete(path) if File.exist?(path)
   321→  end
   322→
   323→  def remove_pubkey(sandbox)
   324→    return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   325→
   326→    begin
   327→      container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   328→      username = sandbox.user.name
   329→      # Match end-of-line to avoid substring collisions
   330→      # (e.g. "wetty-user-foo" must not also remove "wetty-user-foobar").
   331→      # Sandbox names are [a-z0-9_-] only, so safe for regex.
   332→      marker = "wetty-#{sandbox.full_name}$"
   333→      container.exec([
   334→        "sh", "-c",
   335→        "grep -v '#{marker}' /home/#{username}/.ssh/authorized_keys > /tmp/ak_clean && " \
   336→        "mv /tmp/ak_clean /home/#{username}/.ssh/authorized_keys || true"
   337→      ])
   338→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError, Docker::Error::DockerError
   339→      # Sandbox container gone or exec failed — best-effort
   340→    end
   341→  end
   342→end
   343→
```

```
     1→class VncManager
     2→  DATA_DIR = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→  NOVNC_IMAGE = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_NOVNC_IMAGE", "gotget/novnc:latest")
     4→  NETWORK_NAME = "sandcastle-web"
     5→  DYNAMIC_DIR = File.join(DATA_DIR, "traefik", "dynamic")
     6→
     7→  class Error < StandardError; end
     8→
     9→  # Opens a web-based VNC session for the given sandbox.
    10→  # Returns the URL path to the noVNC session.
    11→  def open(sandbox:)
    12→    raise Error, "Sandbox is not running" unless sandbox.status == "running"
    13→    raise Error, "Sandbox has no container" if sandbox.container_id.blank?
    14→
    15→    user = sandbox.user
    16→    container_name = vnc_container_name(sandbox)
    17→
    18→    # Idempotent: if noVNC container already running, return URL
    19→    if container_running?(container_name)
    20→      return vnc_url(sandbox)
    21→    end
    22→
    23→    pull_image
    24→    ensure_network
    25→    connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)
    26→
    27→    # Write Traefik config early so it has time to detect the new route
    28→    write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    29→
    30→    create_novnc_container(sandbox: sandbox, user: user)
    31→
    32→    vnc_url(sandbox)
    33→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    34→    raise Error, "Failed to open browser: #{e.message}"
    35→  rescue SystemCallError => e
    36→    raise Error, "Failed to open browser: #{e.message}"
    37→  end
    38→
    39→  # Closes the web VNC session for the given sandbox.
    40→  def close(sandbox:)
    41→    container_name = vnc_container_name(sandbox)
    42→
    43→    # Stop and remove noVNC container
    44→    begin
    45→      container = Docker::Container.get(container_name)
    46→      container.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
    47→      container.delete(force: true)
    48→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
    49→      # Already gone
    50→    end
    51→
    52→    # Delete Traefik config
    53→    delete_traefik_config(sandbox)
    54→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    55→    Rails.logger.error("VncManager: close failed for #{sandbox.full_name}: #{e.message}")
    56→  end
    57→
    58→  # Returns true if the noVNC container is running AND its HTTP server is ready.
    59→  # The container_running? check alone is insufficient — the websockify process
    60→  # may not have bound to port 6080 yet when Docker reports the container as running.
    61→  def active?(sandbox:)
    62→    container_name = vnc_container_name(sandbox)
    63→    return false unless container_running?(container_name)
    64→
    65→    TCPSocket.new(container_name, 6080).close
    66→    true
    67→  rescue Errno::ECONNREFUSED, Errno::ETIMEDOUT, SocketError
    68→    false
    69→  end
    70→
    71→  # Removes orphaned noVNC containers whose sandbox no longer exists or is not running.
    72→  def cleanup_orphaned
    73→    Docker::Container.all(all: true).each do |container|
    74→      name = container.info.dig("Names")&.first&.delete_prefix("/")
    75→      next unless name&.start_with?("sc-vnc-")
    76→
    77→      labels = container.info["Labels"] || {}
    78→      sandbox_id = labels["sandcastle.sandbox_id"]&.to_i
    79→
    80→      sandbox = sandbox_id ? Sandbox.find_by(id: sandbox_id) : nil
    81→      should_remove = sandbox.nil? || sandbox.status != "running"
    82→
    83→      if should_remove
    84→        container.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
    85→        container.delete(force: true)
    86→        Rails.logger.info("VncManager: removed orphaned noVNC container #{name}")
    87→
    88→        # Clean up Traefik config if we have a sandbox_id
    89→        if sandbox_id
    90→          config_path = File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "vnc-#{sandbox_id}.yml")
    91→          File.delete(config_path) if File.exist?(config_path)
    92→        end
    93→      end
    94→    end
    95→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    96→    Rails.logger.error("VncManager: orphan cleanup failed: #{e.message}")
    97→  end
    98→
    99→  private
   100→
   101→  def vnc_container_name(sandbox)
   102→    "sc-vnc-#{sandbox.full_name}"
   103→  end
   104→
   105→  def vnc_url(sandbox)
   106→    id = sandbox.id
   107→    # Strip prefix is /vnc/{id}, so all requests under /vnc/{id}/* are forwarded
   108→    # to the noVNC container with the prefix removed. This keeps noVNC's relative
   109→    # asset paths (package.json, core/*.js) resolving correctly within the prefix.
   110→    # The ?path= tells noVNC where to connect the WebSocket; Traefik strips
   111→    # /vnc/{id} leaving /websockify which websockify handles.
   112→    "/vnc/#{id}/vnc.html?path=vnc/#{id}/websockify&autoconnect=true"
   113→  end
   114→
   115→  def container_running?(name)
   116→    container = Docker::Container.get(name)
   117→    container.json.dig("State", "Running") == true
   118→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   119→    false
   120→  end
   121→
   122→  def pull_image
   123→    Docker::Image.get(NOVNC_IMAGE)
   124→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   125→    Docker::Image.create("fromImage" => NOVNC_IMAGE)
   126→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   127→    raise Error, "Failed to pull #{NOVNC_IMAGE} — check network connectivity"
   128→  end
   129→
   130→  def ensure_network
   131→    Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   132→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   133→    Docker::Network.create(NETWORK_NAME, "Driver" => "bridge")
   134→  end
   135→
   136→  def connect_sandbox_to_network(sandbox)
   137→    return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   138→
   139→    network = Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   140→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   141→
   142→    networks = container.json.dig("NetworkSettings", "Networks") || {}
   143→    return if networks.key?(NETWORK_NAME)
   144→
   145→    network.connect(sandbox.container_id)
   146→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   147→    # Container no longer exists - sync job will fix the DB state
   148→    raise Error, "Sandbox container not found. Please refresh and try again."
   149→  end
   150→
   151→  def create_novnc_container(sandbox:, user:)
   152→    container_name = vnc_container_name(sandbox)
   153→
   154→    # Remove any existing container with this name
   155→    begin
   156→      old = Docker::Container.get(container_name)
   157→      old.stop(t: 3) rescue nil
   158→      old.delete(force: true)
   159→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   160→      # No existing container
   161→    end
   162→
   163→    host_config = {
   164→      "NetworkMode" => NETWORK_NAME,
   165→      "RestartPolicy" => { "Name" => "no" },
   166→      "Memory" => 128 * 1024 * 1024, # 128MB — noVNC static files + websockify proxy
   167→      "NanoCpus" => 250_000_000 # 0.25 CPU
   168→    }
   169→
   170→    # Run the noVNC image using its built-in launch.sh, which starts both the
   171→    # mini-webserver (noVNC HTML/JS) and websockify (WS→RFB proxy) on port 6080.
   172→    # Traefik routes everything under /vnc/{id} to this container; after
   173→    # stripPrefix the websockify path becomes /websockify as noVNC expects.
   174→    container = Docker::Container.create(
   175→      "name" => container_name,
   176→      "Image" => NOVNC_IMAGE,
   177→      "Cmd" => [ "--vnc", "#{sandbox.full_name}:5900", "--listen", "6080" ],
   178→      "HostConfig" => host_config,
   179→      "Labels" => {
   180→        "sandcastle.sandbox_id" => sandbox.id.to_s,
   181→        "sandcastle.role" => "novnc"
   182→      }
   183→    )
   184→
   185→    container.start
   186→    container
   187→  end
   188→
   189→  def write_traefik_config(sandbox)
   190→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
   191→
   192→    host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "localhost")
   193→    id = sandbox.id
   194→    container_name = vnc_container_name(sandbox)
   195→    sandbox_name   = sandbox.full_name
   196→
   197→    base_rule = if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   198→      "HostRegexp(`.+`) && PathPrefix(`/vnc/#{id}`)"
   199→    else
   200→      "Host(`#{host}`) && PathPrefix(`/vnc/#{id}`)"
   201→    end
   202→
   203→    # Single router: all traffic under /vnc/{id} goes to the noVNC sidecar.
   204→    # The noVNC container (gotget/novnc launch.sh) serves static HTML/JS on /
   205→    # and proxies WebSocket VNC frames via websockify on /websockify, both on
   206→    # port 6080. stripPrefix removes /vnc/{id} before forwarding, so noVNC's
   207→    # relative asset paths (package.json, core/*.js, /websockify) resolve correctly.
   208→    config = {
   209→      "http" => {
   210→        "routers" => {
   211→          "vnc-#{id}" => {
   212→            "rule" => base_rule,
   213→            "service" => "vnc-#{id}",
   214→            "entryPoints" => [ "websecure" ],
   215→            "tls" => tls_config,

   217→            "priority" => 100

   220→        "middlewares" => {
   221→          "vnc-auth-#{id}" => {
   222→            "forwardAuth" => {
   223→              "address" => "http://sandcastle-web:80/vnc/auth",
   224→              "trustForwardHeader" => true
   225→            }
   226→          },
   227→          "vnc-stripprefix-#{id}" => {
   228→            "stripPrefix" => {
   229→              "prefixes" => [ "/vnc/#{id}" ]
   230→            }
   231→          }
   232→        },
   233→        "services" => {
   234→          "vnc-#{id}" => {
   235→            "loadBalancer" => {
   236→              "servers" => [ { "url" => "http://#{container_name}:6080" } ]
   237→            }
   238→          }
   239→        }
   240→      }
   241→    }
   242→
   243→    File.write(File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "vnc-#{id}.yml"), config.to_yaml)
   244→  end
   245→
   246→  def tls_config
   247→    if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   248→      {}
   249→    else
   250→      { "certResolver" => "letsencrypt" }
   251→    end
   252→  end

   255→    path = File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "vnc-#{sandbox.id}.yml")
   256→    File.delete(path) if File.exist?(path)
```

```
     1→class TerminalController < ApplicationController
     2→  allow_unauthenticated_access only: :auth
     3→  skip_before_action :require_password_change, only: :auth
     4→  skip_forgery_protection only: :open
     5→
     6→  def open
     7→    sandbox = find_sandbox
     8→
     9→    TerminalManager.new.open(sandbox: sandbox)
    10→    redirect_to terminal_wait_sandbox_path(sandbox), status: :see_other
    11→  rescue TerminalManager::Error => e
    12→    redirect_to root_path, alert: e.message
    13→  end
    14→
    15→  def wait
    16→    @sandbox = find_sandbox
    17→    @terminal_url = terminal_redirect_url("/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/wetty")
    18→  end
    19→
    20→  def status
    21→    sandbox = find_sandbox
    22→    ready = TerminalManager.new.active?(sandbox: sandbox)
    23→    render json: { status: ready ? "ready" : "waiting" }
    24→  end
    25→
    26→  def close
    27→    sandbox = find_sandbox
    28→
    29→    TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    30→    redirect_to root_path, notice: "Terminal closed"
    31→  rescue TerminalManager::Error => e
    32→    redirect_to root_path, alert: e.message
    33→  end
    34→
    35→  private
    36→
    37→  def find_sandbox
    38→    if Current.user.admin?
    39→      Sandbox.active.find(params[:id])
    40→    else
    41→      Current.user.sandboxes.active.find(params[:id])
    42→    end
    43→  end
    44→
    45→  # Build the full terminal URL. In production, Traefik is the entry point
    46→  # so a relative path works. In local dev (selfsigned TLS), Rails may be
    47→  # accessed directly on a different port, so we need an absolute URL.
    48→  def terminal_redirect_url(path)
    49→    base = ENV["SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL"]
    50→    base ? "#{base}#{path}" : path
    51→  end
    52→
    53→  public
    54→
    55→  # Called by Traefik forwardAuth. Returns 200 to allow, or redirects to
    56→  # login (which Traefik passes through to the browser).
    57→  def auth
    58→    forwarded_uri = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Uri"] || ""
    59→    match = forwarded_uri.match(%r{/terminal/(\d+)/wetty})
    60→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless match
    61→
    62→    session_record = find_session_by_cookie
    63→    unless session_record
    64→      # Build the original terminal URL from Traefik's forwarded headers
    65→      # so the user returns here after logging in.
    66→      proto = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Proto"] || "https"
    67→      host  = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Host"] || request.host_with_port
    68→      original_url = "#{proto}://#{host}#{forwarded_uri}"
    69→      session[:return_to_after_authenticating] = original_url
    70→      redirect_to new_session_url, allow_other_host: true
    71→      return
    72→    end
    73→
    74→    user = session_record.user
    75→    sandbox = Sandbox.active.find_by(id: match[1].to_i)
    76→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless sandbox && (sandbox.user_id == user.id || user.admin?)
    77→
    78→    head :ok
    79→  end
    80→end
    81→
```

```
    17→    id = @sandbox.id
    18→    @vnc_url = vnc_redirect_url("/vnc/#{id}/vnc.html?path=vnc/#{id}/websockify&autoconnect=true")

    21→  def status

    32→  rescue VncManager::Error => e
    33→    redirect_to root_path, alert: e.message
    34→  end
    35→
    36→  private
    37→
    38→  def find_sandbox
    39→    if Current.user.admin?
    40→      Sandbox.active.find(params[:id])
    41→    else
    42→      Current.user.sandboxes.active.find(params[:id])
    43→    end
    44→  end
    45→
    46→  # Build the full VNC URL. In production, Traefik is the entry point
    47→  # so a relative path works. In local dev (selfsigned TLS), Rails may be
    48→  # accessed directly on a different port, so we need an absolute URL.
    49→  def vnc_redirect_url(path)
    50→    base = ENV["SANDCASTLE_VNC_URL"] || ENV["SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL"]
    51→    base ? "#{base}#{path}" : path
    52→  end
    53→
    54→  public
    55→
    56→  # Called by Traefik forwardAuth. Returns 200 to allow, or redirects to
    57→  # login (which Traefik passes through to the browser).
    58→  def auth
    59→    forwarded_uri = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Uri"] || ""
    60→    match = forwarded_uri.match(%r{/vnc/(\d+)})
    61→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless match
    62→
    63→    session_record = find_session_by_cookie
    64→    unless session_record
    65→      # Build the original VNC URL from Traefik's forwarded headers
    66→      # so the user returns here after logging in.
    67→      proto = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Proto"] || "https"
    68→      host  = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Host"] || request.host_with_port
    69→      original_url = "#{proto}://#{host}#{forwarded_uri}"
    70→      session[:return_to_after_authenticating] = original_url
    71→      redirect_to new_session_url, allow_other_host: true
    72→      return
    73→    end
    74→
    75→    user = session_record.user
    76→    sandbox = Sandbox.active.find_by(id: match[1].to_i)
    77→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless sandbox && (sandbox.user_id == user.id || user.admin?)
    78→
    79→    head :ok
    80→  end
    81→end
    82→
```

> AGENT

Found it. The polling interval is **1000ms** in both wait pages, and crucially `setInterval` doesn't fire immediately — so the first check is always at least 1 second after page load. By that point if the container isn't ready, it's another full second before the next check. That's your 2-second delay.

**The fix:** poll immediately on load + use 250ms interval + use wall-clock timeout. Let me apply it to both views:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01PPKWrYhxR4NjEgvRiqkKmc
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/terminal/wait.html.erb",
  "old_string": "<script>\n  (function() {\n    var statusUrl = \"<%= terminal_status_sandbox_path(@sandbox) %>\";\n    var terminalUrl = \"<%= j @terminal_url %>\";\n    var elapsed = 0;\n    var timeout = 30;\n\n    var poll = setInterval(function() {\n      elapsed++;\n\n      if (elapsed > timeout) {\n        clearInterval(poll);\n        document.getElementById(\"connecting\").classList.add(\"hidden\");\n        document.getElementById(\"timeout-error\").classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n        return;\n      }\n\n      fetch(statusUrl, { headers: { \"Accept\": \"application/json\" } })\n        .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })\n        .then(function(data) {\n          if (data.status === \"ready\") {\n            clearInterval(poll);\n            window.location.href = terminalUrl;\n          }\n        })\n        .catch(function() {});\n    }, 1000);\n  })();\n</script>",
  "new_string": "<script>\n  (function() {\n    var statusUrl = \"<%= terminal_status_sandbox_path(@sandbox) %>\";\n    var terminalUrl = \"<%= j @terminal_url %>\";\n    var startTime = Date.now();\n    var TIMEOUT_MS = 30000;\n    var poll;\n\n    function check() {\n      if (Date.now() - startTime > TIMEOUT_MS) {\n        clearInterval(poll);\n        document.getElementById(\"connecting\").classList.add(\"hidden\");\n        document.getElementById(\"timeout-error\").classList.remove(\"hidden\");\n        return;\n      }\n\n      fetch(statusUrl, { headers: { \"Accept\": \"application/json\" } })\n        .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })\n        .then(function(data) {\n          if (data.status === \"ready\") {\n            clearInterval(poll);\n            window.location.href = terminalUrl;\n          }\n        })\n        .catch(function() {});\n    }\n\n    check();\n    poll = setInterval(check, 250);\n  })();\n</script>"
}
```

> AGENT

Deployed. Here's what I found and fixed:

**Root cause:** Both wait pages used `setInterval(..., 1000)` — which means the **first status check fires 1 second after page load**. If the container isn't ready in that exact window, you wait another full second for the next check. Hence the consistent ~2s delay.

**Fix applied to both `terminal/wait.html.erb` and `vnc/wait.html.erb`:**
- `check()` fires **immediately** on page load (no initial wait)
- Then polls every **250ms** instead of 1000ms
- Timeout uses wall clock (`Date.now()`) instead of a counter — still 30s

Try opening a terminal and VNC again — you should see it connect in ~250ms of the container being ready rather than the next 1s tick.

> TOOL

> AGENT

Done. The logic is now:

1. `open` creates/starts the container
2. Immediately checks `active?` (TCPSocket connect — fast, either connects or gets ECONNREFUSED instantly)
3. **If ready → redirect straight to the terminal/VNC URL** (no wait page at all)
4. **If not ready yet → show wait page** (which now polls at 250ms)

For the idempotent case (container already running) you'll go straight through. For a fresh container you'll only see the wait page for the ~250ms it takes WeTTY/noVNC to bind their port.

> AGENT

Found the problem. **HTTP 304 caching.** The browser caches the `"waiting"` status response and keeps getting 304s instead of re-fetching — so it never sees `"ready"` until the response body actually changes. Look at the pattern:

```
17:41:54.470 → status 200 "waiting"  (cached)
17:41:54.718 → status 304            (browser uses cached "waiting")
17:41:54.963 → status 304            (browser uses cached "waiting")
17:41:55.213 → status 304            (browser uses cached "waiting")
17:41:55.463 → status 200 "ready"    (finally different content, cache miss)
```

Fix: disable caching on the status actions server-side:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_012P6EgKh926MppXUAFvAett
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb",
  "old_string": "  def status\n    sandbox = find_sandbox\n    ready = TerminalManager.new.active?(sandbox: sandbox)\n    render json: { status: ready ? \"ready\" : \"waiting\" }\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def status\n    sandbox = find_sandbox\n    response.headers[\"Cache-Control\"] = \"no-store\"\n    ready = TerminalManager.new.active?(sandbox: sandbox)\n    render json: { status: ready ? \"ready\" : \"waiting\" }\n  end"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. The 304s were the real culprit — Rails auto-generates ETags for JSON responses, browser caches the `"waiting"` body, and subsequent polls get 304 (cached "waiting") until the body changes to `"ready"`. Adding `Cache-Control: no-store` forces every poll to be a fresh fetch. No restart needed — Rails picks up the file change on the next request.

> AGENT

The 942ms is the controller spending ~900ms doing all the Docker work synchronously before even responding to the browser. The fix: kick off the container creation in a background job and respond immediately. The wait page then does its polling job.

> TOOL

```json
}
```

```
    15→    end
    16→  rescue TerminalManager::Error => e
    17→    redirect_to root_path, alert: e.message
    18→  end
    19→
    20→  def wait
    21→    @sandbox = find_sandbox
    22→    @terminal_url = terminal_redirect_url("/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/wetty")
    23→  end
    24→
    25→  def status
    26→    sandbox = find_sandbox
    27→    response.headers["Cache-Control"] = "no-store"
    28→    ready = TerminalManager.new.active?(sandbox: sandbox)
    29→    render json: { status: ready ? "ready" : "waiting" }
    30→  end
    31→
    32→  def close
    33→    sandbox = find_sandbox
    34→
    35→    TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    36→    redirect_to root_path, notice: "Terminal closed"
    37→  rescue TerminalManager::Error => e
    38→    redirect_to root_path, alert: e.message
    39→  end
    40→
    41→  private
    42→
    43→  def find_sandbox
    44→    if Current.user.admin?
    45→      Sandbox.active.find(params[:id])
    46→    else
    47→      Current.user.sandboxes.active.find(params[:id])
    48→    end
    49→  end
    50→
    51→  # Build the full terminal URL. In production, Traefik is the entry point
    52→  # so a relative path works. In local dev (selfsigned TLS), Rails may be
    53→  # accessed directly on a different port, so we need an absolute URL.
    54→  def terminal_redirect_url(path)
    55→    base = ENV["SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL"]
    56→    base ? "#{base}#{path}" : path
    57→  end
    58→
    59→  public
    60→
    61→  # Called by Traefik forwardAuth. Returns 200 to allow, or redirects to
    62→  # login (which Traefik passes through to the browser).
    63→  def auth
    64→    forwarded_uri = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Uri"] || ""
    65→    match = forwarded_uri.match(%r{/terminal/(\d+)/wetty})
    66→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless match
    67→
    68→    session_record = find_session_by_cookie
    69→    unless session_record
    70→      # Build the original terminal URL from Traefik's forwarded headers
    71→      # so the user returns here after logging in.
    72→      proto = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Proto"] || "https"
    73→      host  = request.headers["X-Forwarded-Host"] || request.host_with_port
    74→      original_url = "#{proto}://#{host}#{forwarded_uri}"
    75→      session[:return_to_after_authenticating] = original_url
    76→      redirect_to new_session_url, allow_other_host: true
    77→      return
    78→    end
    79→
    80→    user = session_record.user
    81→    sandbox = Sandbox.active.find_by(id: match[1].to_i)
    82→    head(:unauthorized) and return unless sandbox && (sandbox.user_id == user.id || user.admin?)
    83→
    84→    head :ok
    85→  end
    86→end
    87→
```

Now I have the full picture. The fix:
- **Terminal**: use the existing `TerminalOpenJob` instead of calling `open` synchronously
- **VNC**: create `VncOpenJob` + same pattern

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/jobs/vnc_open_job.rb",
  "content": "class VncOpenJob < ApplicationJob\n  queue_as :default\n\n  def perform(sandbox_id:)\n    sandbox = Sandbox.find(sandbox_id)\n    return unless sandbox.status == \"running\"\n\n    sandbox.start_job(\"opening_vnc\")\n\n    begin\n      VncManager.new.open(sandbox: sandbox)\n      sandbox.finish_job\n    rescue => e\n      Rails.logger.error(\"VncOpenJob failed: #{e.message}\\n#{e.backtrace.join(\"\\n\")}\")\n      sandbox.fail_job(\"Failed to open browser: #{e.message}\")\n      # Don't re-raise - VNC failures shouldn't retry\n    end\n  end\nend\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Now the flow is:

**Before:** POST takes 942ms (synchronous Docker work) → wait page → more waiting  
**After:** POST takes ~10ms (just DB lookup + job enqueue) → wait page appears instantly → background job does Docker work → 250ms poll detects ready

The wait page will still show for ~1s while the job runs, but the POST response is instant — the browser gets there immediately.

> AGENT

Two issues in the logs:

1. **`/terminal/28/wetty` → 404 in Rails**: Traefik wrote `Host("localhost")` in the route — the worker container doesn't have `SANDCASTLE_HOST` set, so `write_traefik_config` defaults to `localhost` instead of `hq.sandcastle.rocks`
2. **Same for VNC**: `Host("localhost") && PathPrefix("/vnc/28")`

The Traefik config must be written by the web container (which has the right env vars), not the worker. Let me check the docker-compose to understand the env setup, then fix the architecture:

> TOOL

```json
  "command": "grep -A5 \"sandcastle-worker\" /Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/docker-compose.yml | head -30",
  "description": "Check worker env vars in docker-compose"
}
```

> AGENT

The worker has no `SANDCASTLE_HOST` env var — it defaults to `localhost`. The fix has two parts:
1. **Permanent**: add `SANDCASTLE_HOST` to worker in docker-compose
2. **Immediate**: write the Traefik config from the web container (controller) before the job runs, and guard against the job overwriting it

> AGENT

All done. Summary of what was wrong and what's fixed:

**Root cause:** The worker container was missing `SANDCASTLE_HOST`, so `write_traefik_config` wrote `Host("localhost")` → Traefik couldn't match `hq.sandcastle.rocks` → fell through to Rails → 404.

**Fixes applied:**
1. **Immediate**: Controllers now call `prepare_traefik_config` from the web container (which has the correct `SANDCASTLE_HOST`) *before* enqueueing the job. The `write_traefik_config` method now skips if the file already exists, so the worker can't overwrite it with `localhost`.
2. **Permanent**: `docker-compose.yml` now passes `SANDCASTLE_HOST` and `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE` to the worker — will take effect on next deploy.
3. **Cleanup**: Deleted the bad `terminal-28.yml` and `vnc-28.yml` Traefik configs.

Try opening WeTTY/VNC again now.

> AGENT

All files patched and both containers restarted. Here's what was deployed:

- **Controllers** (`terminal_controller.rb`, `vnc_controller.rb`) → `sandcastle-web`
- **Services** (`terminal_manager.rb`, `vnc_manager.rb`) → both containers
- **Jobs** (`vnc_open_job.rb`) → both containers
- **Views** (`terminal/wait.html.erb`, `vnc/wait.html.erb`) → `sandcastle-web`

The patch brings all the fixes live: immediate `active?` check on open (skip wait page if ready), background job enqueueing (~30ms POST response), Traefik config written by web container before job enqueue, `File.exist?` guard preventing worker overwrite, and 250ms polling with `Cache-Control: no-store`.

```
     1→class Setting < ApplicationRecord
     2→  encrypts :github_client_secret, :google_client_secret, :smtp_password
     3→
     4→  def self.instance
     5→    find_or_create_by(id: 1)
     6→  end
     7→
     8→  # --- Class-level accessors with ENV fallback ---
     9→
    10→  %i[github_client_id google_client_id].each do |attr|
    11→    define_singleton_method(attr) do
    12→      val = instance.send(attr)
    13→      val.presence || ENV[attr.to_s.upcase]
    14→    end
    15→  end
    16→
    17→  %i[github_client_secret google_client_secret].each do |attr|
    18→    define_singleton_method(attr) do
    19→      val = instance.send(attr)
    20→      val.presence || ENV[attr.to_s.upcase]
    21→    end
    22→  end
    23→
    24→  def self.github_configured?
    25→    github_client_id.present? && github_client_secret.present?
    26→  end
    27→
    28→  def self.google_configured?
    29→    google_client_id.present? && google_client_secret.present?
    30→  end
    31→
    32→  def self.smtp_configured?
    33→    instance.smtp_address.present?
    34→  end
    35→
    36→  def self.smtp_from_address
    37→    val = instance.smtp_from_address
    38→    val.presence || ENV["SMTP_FROM_ADDRESS"] || "noreply@example.com"
    39→  end
    40→
    41→  # Skip blank secret values so "leave blank to keep" works
    42→  %i[github_client_secret google_client_secret smtp_password].each do |attr|
    43→    define_method(:"#{attr}=") do |value|
    44→      super(value) if value.present?
    45→    end
    46→  end
    47→
    48→  def smtp_settings
    49→    return {} unless smtp_address.present?
    50→
    51→    settings = {
    52→      address: smtp_address,
    53→      port: smtp_port || 587,
    54→      user_name: smtp_username.presence,
    55→      password: smtp_password.presence,
    56→      authentication: smtp_authentication.presence&.to_sym,
    57→      enable_starttls_auto: smtp_starttls
    58→    }
    59→    settings.compact
    60→  end
    61→end
```

> AGENT

The issue: `@setting.smtp_password.present?` (and same for the OAuth secrets) triggers decryption, which fails because the value was encrypted with a different `RAILS_MASTER_KEY`. Two fixes needed:

1. Add `*_set?` instance methods on the model that rescue decryption errors
2. Update the view to use those methods
3. Also guard the class-level secret accessors

> TOOL

```json
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/models/setting.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Skip blank secret values so \"leave blank to keep\" works\n  %i[github_client_secret google_client_secret smtp_password].each do |attr|\n    define_method(:\"#{attr}=\") do |value|\n      super(value) if value.present?\n    end\n  end",
  "new_string": "  # Skip blank secret values so \"leave blank to keep\" works\n  %i[github_client_secret google_client_secret smtp_password].each do |attr|\n    define_method(:\"#{attr}=\") do |value|\n      super(value) if value.present?\n    end\n  end\n\n  # Returns true if the encrypted attribute has a stored value, even if it\n  # cannot be decrypted with the current key (e.g. after a key rotation).\n  %i[github_client_secret google_client_secret smtp_password].each do |attr|\n    define_method(:\"#{attr}_set?\") do\n      send(attr).present?\n    rescue ActiveRecord::Encryption::Errors::Decryption\n      true\n    end\n  end"
}
```

> AGENT

Clear picture now:
- Credentials only have `secret_key_base` (overridden by env anyway), no AR encryption keys
- `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` is never passed to production containers
- AR Encryption falls back to deriving keys from the master key locally → different keys in production → decryption fails

Plan:
1. Generate AR encryption keys via `rails db:encryption:init` and add to credentials
2. Commit `credentials.yml.enc`
3. Add `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` to docker-compose.yml (web, worker, migrate)
4. Update installer to generate/persist `RAILS_MASTER_KEY`
5. Update `install-defaults`

> TOOL

```json
  "new_string": "    SECRET_KEY_BASE=$(openssl rand -hex 64)\n    DOCKER_GID=$(stat -c '%g' \"$DOCKER_SOCK\" 2>/dev/null || echo \"988\")\n\n    # Preserve DB password across reinstalls (user data)\n    POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE=\"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres/.secrets\"\n    if [ -f \"$POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE\" ]; then\n      # shellcheck source=/dev/null\n      source \"$POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      info \"Reusing existing database password\"\n    else\n      DB_PASSWORD=$(openssl rand -hex 32)\n      mkdir -p \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres\"\n      echo \"DB_PASSWORD=$DB_PASSWORD\" > \"$POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      chmod 600 \"$POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      wrote \"$POSTGRES_SECRETS_FILE\"\n    fi\n\n    # Preserve RAILS_MASTER_KEY across reinstalls — required to decrypt\n    # config/credentials.yml.enc (which holds the AR encryption keys).\n    RAILS_SECRETS_FILE=\"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/rails/.secrets\"\n    if [ -f \"$RAILS_SECRETS_FILE\" ]; then\n      # shellcheck source=/dev/null\n      source \"$RAILS_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      info \"Reusing existing Rails master key\"\n    else\n      mkdir -p \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/rails\"\n      RAILS_MASTER_KEY=$(openssl rand -hex 16)\n      echo \"RAILS_MASTER_KEY=$RAILS_MASTER_KEY\" > \"$RAILS_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      chmod 600 \"$RAILS_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      wrote \"$RAILS_SECRETS_FILE\"\n      warn \"RAILS_MASTER_KEY generated. ```json
```
```json
{
  "command": "gh issue create \\\n  --title \"Multi-install AR encryption key stability\" \\\n  --body \"## Problem\n\nWhen Sandcastle is installed fresh on a new machine (or with a new \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\`), any encrypted settings stored in the database (SMTP password, OAuth secrets) become unrecoverable — decryption fails with \\`ActiveRecord::Encryption::Errors::Decryption\\` because the AR encryption keys are derived from a different master key.\n\nThis also means encrypted values written on one install cannot be read by another install of the same codebase (e.g., staging vs production, or after a reinstall).\n\n## Root Cause\n\n- \\`config/credentials.yml.enc\\` holds the AR encryption keys (\\`primary_key\\`, \\`deterministic_key\\`, \\`key_derivation_salt\\`) but \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` was never passed to production containers.\n- Without \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\`, Rails falls back to deriving AR encryption keys from the master key locally — keys that differ per environment.\n\n## Fix Landed (partial)\n\n- AR encryption keys generated via \\`rails db:encryption:init\\` and stored in \\`config/credentials.yml.enc\\`\n- \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` now passed to all containers in \\`docker-compose.yml\\`\n- Installer generates and persists \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` in \\`data/rails/.secrets\\` (like \\`DB_PASSWORD\\`)\n\n## Remaining Work / Open Questions\n\n- [ ] **Multi-install key sharing**: If an operator installs Sandcastle on machine B and wants to read encrypted values from a database dump taken on machine A, they need to copy \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` (and therefore \\`credentials.yml.enc\\`) from A to B. There is currently no documented procedure or tooling for this.\n- [ ] **Key rotation**: If the master key is lost, there is no recovery path for encrypted values. Should we store a plaintext indicator that an encrypted value exists (so the UI can show \\\"value set, but unreadable\\\" instead of 500)?\n- [ ] **Backup docs**: The installer warns on first run but there is no backup/restore section in the guide covering \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` + \\`credentials.yml.enc\\`.\n- [ ] **Alternative**: Consider moving AR encryption keys out of credentials into installer-generated env vars (\\`AR_ENCRYPTION_PRIMARY_KEY\\` etc.), making the system key-rotation-friendly without needing Rails credentials at all. This would decouple AR encryption from \\`RAILS_MASTER_KEY\\` entirely.\n\" \\\n  --label \"bug,enhancement\" 2>&1 || \\\ngh issue create \\\n  --title \"Multi-install AR encryption key stability\" \\\n  --body \"## Problem\n\nWhen Sandcastle is installed fresh on a new machine (or with a new RAILS_MASTER_KEY), any encrypted settings stored in the database (SMTP password, OAuth secrets) become unrecoverable.\n\n## Root Cause\n\nconfig/credentials.yml.enc holds the AR encryption keys but RAILS_MASTER_KEY was never passed to production containers. Without it, Rails derives AR encryption keys from the local master key — keys that differ per environment.\n\n## Fix Landed (partial)\n\n- AR encryption keys generated via rails db:encryption:init and stored in credentials.yml.enc\n- RAILS_MASTER_KEY now passed to all containers in docker-compose.yml\n- Installer generates and persists RAILS_MASTER_KEY in data/rails/.secrets (like DB_PASSWORD)\n\n## Remaining Work\n\n- [ ] Multi-install key sharing: no documented procedure for copying RAILS_MASTER_KEY between installs to share a database\n- [ ] Key loss recovery: if master key is lost, encrypted values are unrecoverable. UI should show 'value set but unreadable' instead of 500.\n- [ ] Backup docs: no backup/restore section covering RAILS_MASTER_KEY + credentials.yml.enc\n- [ ] Alternative: move AR encryption keys to installer-generated env vars (AR_ENCRYPTION_PRIMARY_KEY etc.) to decouple from RAILS_MASTER_KEY entirely\n\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create GitHub issue for multi-install AR encryption"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Here's what was done:

**Changes committed:**
- `config/credentials.yml.enc` — AR encryption keys (`primary_key`, `deterministic_key`, `key_derivation_salt`) generated via `rails db:encryption:init`
- `docker-compose.yml` — `RAILS_MASTER_KEY: ${RAILS_MASTER_KEY}` added to web, worker, and migrate services
- `installer/installer.sh.in` + `installer.sh` — generates `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` on first install, persists it in `data/rails/.secrets`, reuses on reinstall (same pattern as `DB_PASSWORD`)
- `install-defaults` — documents the new variable with a backup warning

**To deploy to sandman:**
1. Get your local master key: `cat config/master.key`
2. Add `RAILS_MASTER_KEY=<that value>` to sandman's `.env` file
3. Redeploy with `docker-compose up -d`

**GitHub issue #41** filed for the multi-install follow-up (key sharing across installs, backup docs, alternative of using plain env vars for AR keys).

> AGENT

Same situation — just overflowed the 2.4MB task buffer. The logs are clean: normal dashboard requests, `/settings` returning 200, WebSocket connections. Nothing alarming. Both monitors just hit the output size limit and exited with code 255.

> DEVELOPER

should the (primary_key, deterministic_key, key_derivation_salt) also be generated on install?

> AGENT

Yes — and that's actually the better approach. The keys currently baked into `credentials.yml.enc` are **identical for every Sandcastle install** (they're in the repo), so `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` only adds ceremony without real protection. Per-install generated env vars give each deployment unique keys.

The plan:
1. Remove `active_record_encryption` block from `credentials.yml.enc`
2. Add a Rails initializer to configure AR encryption from env vars
3. Installer generates the 3 keys (like `SECRET_KEY_BASE`) and writes them to `.env`
4. `docker-compose.yml` passes them to containers
5. Revert `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` from docker-compose (credentials only has `secret_key_base` which is already overridden by env anyway)

> TOOL

```json
  "description": "Remove active_record_encryption block from credentials"
}
```

> AGENT

Yes — and that's the right call. Here's why the previous approach was wrong:

**Before:** Keys baked into `credentials.yml.enc` → same keys for every Sandcastle install → no real isolation between deployments, and anyone cloning the repo had the keys.

**Now:**
- Installer generates 3 unique keys per install via `openssl rand -hex 16`, stores them in `data/rails/.secrets` (600 perms, preserved across reinstalls like `DB_PASSWORD`)
- `config/initializers/active_record_encryption.rb` reads them from env at boot
- `docker-compose.yml` passes `AR_ENCRYPTION_PRIMARY_KEY`, `AR_ENCRYPTION_DETERMINISTIC_KEY`, `AR_ENCRYPTION_KEY_DERIVATION_SALT` to all containers
- `RAILS_MASTER_KEY` removed — credentials only hold `secret_key_base` which is already superseded by the `SECRET_KEY_BASE` env var

Each install now has fully isolated encryption. Issue #41 closed.

> DEVELOPER

you are a Sowtfare Architect with Stregth in deligvering shippable web-appt to host yourself. How would you desing the install and upgrade process of sandcstle?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me think through this properly.

---

## Install & Upgrade Architecture for Sandcastle

### The core tension

Self-hosted software has two competing constraints: **operator simplicity** (one command, just works) and **production safety** (don't destroy data, support rollback). Most self-hosted projects fail one or both.

---

### Phase 1: Bootstrap (machine-level, one-time)

This is what `bootstrap/sandcastle-bootstrap.sh` does today. Keep it separate and minimal — it only installs host dependencies (Docker, Sysbox, UFW) that don't change between Sandcastle versions. Importantly, **it should be idempotent**: running it twice on an already-bootstrapped machine should be a no-op.

Current gap: bootstrap and install are conflated in `installer.sh`.

---

### Phase 2: Install

The install command today is essentially `sudo installer.sh install`. The shape is right, but I'd restructure the responsibilities:

```
sandcastle install
  1. validate config (SANDCASTLE_HOST, admin creds)
  2. generate secrets (once, preserved on reinstall)
  3. write .env
  4. pull image
  5. docker-compose up -d
  6. wait for health check → /up
  7. seed admin user
```

**Key principle:** Secrets are generated exactly once and never overwritten. Today we do this for `DB_PASSWORD` and the AR encryption keys. `SECRET_KEY_BASE` should join them — right now it's regenerated on every reinstall, which invalidates all existing sessions.

---

### Phase 3: Upgrade — the hard part

This is almost entirely missing today. Upgrade should be:

```
sandcastle upgrade
  1. check current version (stored in $SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/version)
  2. pull ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:latest (or :vX.Y.Z)
  3. auto-backup (DB dump + secrets)
  4. run migrations via migrate service
  5. restart web + worker
  6. health check with timeout
  7. on failure: restore previous image, restart, alert
```

The **auto-backup before upgrade** is non-negotiable. Operators will run upgrades without reading changelogs.

**Atomic swap pattern:**

```bash
docker pull ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:latest
# tag current as :previous before switching
docker tag ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:current ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:previous
# run migrations
docker-compose run --rm migrate
# rolling restart (keeps DB/worker up)
docker-compose up -d --no-deps web worker
# health check
if ! wait_for_healthy; then
  docker tag ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:previous ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle:current
  docker-compose up -d --no-deps web worker
  echo "Upgrade failed — rolled back to previous version"
fi
```

---

### The .env migration problem

Every new Sandcastle release can add new env vars. Today, an upgrade leaves the existing `.env` untouched — new vars simply don't exist, which causes silent failures or 500s.

The right pattern: **merge, don't overwrite**.

```
$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env          ← operator-set values (never touched on upgrade)
$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env.defaults ← shipped defaults (overwritten on upgrade)
```

The app reads both, with `env` taking precedence. On upgrade, only `env.defaults` is updated. New vars get their defaults automatically; operator overrides survive.

This is analogous to how `/etc/default/` works on Debian systems.

---

### Version tracking

Currently there's no record of what version is installed. Add a `version` file written on install/upgrade:

```
$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/version   ← "v0.2.3"
```

This enables:
- `sandcastle status` to show installed vs latest
- upgrade scripts to handle version-specific migrations (rare but necessary for breaking changes)
- support requests ("what version are you on?")

---

### Backup as a first-class operation

```
sandcastle backup    → $SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/backups/YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM/
  - db.dump          (pg_dump)
  - rails.secrets    (AR encryption keys)
  - postgres.secrets (DB password)
  - env              (full .env snapshot)
```

Auto-runs before every upgrade. Retention: keep last 5.

**Restore:**
```
sandcastle restore 2026-02-18_14-30
```

This is the missing piece that makes key loss recoverable.

---

### What I'd build next (priority order)

| Priority | Work | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `sandcastle upgrade` command | Core missing capability |
| 2 | `.env` split (operator vs defaults) | Prevents silent failures on upgrade |
| 3 | `SECRET_KEY_BASE` moved to `.secrets` | Stops session invalidation on reinstall |
| 4 | `sandcastle backup` / `restore` | Makes key loss recoverable |
| 5 | Version file + `sandcastle status` | Basic operational visibility |
| 6 | Health check gate in upgrade | Enables safe rollback |

---

### What's already good

- The `data/postgres/.secrets` and `data/rails/.secrets` pattern for secret persistence is solid — just needs to cover `SECRET_KEY_BASE` too
- Separating the `migrate` service in docker-compose is correct (migrations run before traffic switches)
- The installer being a single downloadable shell script is the right UX for self-hosted

The overall shape is right. The gaps are in the **upgrade lifecycle**, **secret durability**, and **operational tooling** (`status`, `backup`, `restore`).

```json
{
  "command": "gh issue create \\\n  --title \"feat: comprehensive install & upgrade lifecycle\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Overview\n\nThe current install process works for a fresh deployment but the **upgrade lifecycle is almost entirely missing**. This issue tracks the work needed to make Sandcastle production-safe to operate long-term.\n\n## Current state\n\n| Capability | Status |\n|---|---|\n| Fresh install (`installer.sh install`) | ✅ Works |\n| Secret persistence across reinstall (DB password, AR keys) | ✅ Works |\n| Upgrade command | ❌ Missing |\n| Rollback on failed upgrade | ❌ Missing |\n| New env var propagation on upgrade | ❌ Missing |\n| Backup before upgrade | ❌ Missing |\n| Version tracking | ❌ Missing |\n| `sandcastle status` | ❌ Missing |\n\n## Design\n\n### Two-phase separation\n\n**Bootstrap** (machine-level, one-time):\nInstalls host dependencies — Docker, Sysbox, UFW. Already exists as `bootstrap/sandcastle-bootstrap.sh`. Should be fully idempotent and strictly separated from app install.\n\n**Install / Upgrade** (app-level, repeatable):\nEverything in `$SANDCASTLE_HOME`. Should be re-runnable safely.\n\n---\n\n### Install flow (refined)\n\n```\nsandcastle install\n  1. validate config (SANDCASTLE_HOST, admin creds)\n  2. generate secrets — once, never overwritten on reinstall\n       DB_PASSWORD             → data/postgres/.secrets\n       AR_ENCRYPTION_*         → data/rails/.secrets\n       SECRET_KEY_BASE         → data/rails/.secrets  ← move here (currently regenerated on reinstall, invalidating sessions)\n  3. write .env from env.defaults + operator overrides\n  4. pull image\n  5. docker-compose up -d\n  6. wait for /up health check\n  7. seed admin user\n```\n\n---\n\n### Upgrade flow\n\n```\nsandcastle upgrade [--version vX.Y.Z]\n  1. read installed version from $SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/version\n  2. pull new image\n  3. auto-backup (DB dump + secrets snapshot)\n  4. tag current image as :previous\n  5. docker-compose run --rm migrate   (migrations before traffic)\n  6. docker-compose up -d --no-deps web worker\n  7. health check with 30s timeout → GET /up\n  8. on success: write new version to etc/version\n  9. on failure: retag :previous as current, restart, print rollback notice\n```\n\n**Atomic rollback pattern:**\n```bash\ndocker tag sandcastle:current sandcastle:previous\n# ... upgrade attempt ...\n# on failure:\ndocker tag sandcastle:previous sandcastle:current\ndocker-compose up -d --no-deps web worker\n```\n\n---\n\n### .env split — prevents silent failures on upgrade\n\nToday a fresh `.env` is written on install and never touched again. New env vars added in future releases are silently absent after upgrade.\n\n**Solution: split into two files with merge semantics:**\n\n```\n$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env.defaults   ← shipped defaults, overwritten on every upgrade\n$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/env            ← operator overrides, never touched on upgrade\n```\n\nApp (and docker-compose) reads both, with `env` taking precedence. Analogous to `/etc/default/` on Debian.\n\nOn upgrade, only `env.defaults` is refreshed. New variables get sensible defaults automatically; operator values survive intact.\n\n---\n\n### Version tracking\n\nWrite the installed version on every install/upgrade:\n\n```\n$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/version   # e.g. \"v0.2.3\"\n```\n\nEnables:\n- `sandcastle status` to compare installed vs latest\n- Version-specific upgrade hooks for breaking changes\n- Useful signal in bug reports\n\n---\n\n### Backup & restore\n\nAuto-runs before every upgrade. Also available as a standalone command.\n\n```\nsandcastle backup\n  → $SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/backups/YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM/\n      db.dump           pg_dump of sandcastle_production\n      rails.secrets     AR encryption keys\n      postgres.secrets  DB password\n      env               full .env snapshot\n```\n\nRetention: keep last 5 backups automatically.\n\n```\nsandcastle restore 2026-02-18_14-30\n  1. confirm with operator\n  2. stop web + worker\n  3. pg_restore\n  4. restore secrets files\n  5. restart\n```\n\nThis is the missing recovery path if encryption keys are lost or a bad migration corrupts data.\n\n---\n\n### `sandcastle status`\n\n```\n$ sandcastle status\n\nSandcastle v0.2.3  (latest: v0.2.5 — upgrade available)\nHost:     hq.example.com\nTLS:      letsencrypt\n\nServices:\n  web        running  (sandcastle-web)\n  worker     running  (sandcastle-worker)\n  postgres   running  (sandcastle-postgres)\n  traefik    running\n\nSandboxes: 4 active / 12 total\nLast backup: 2026-02-19 08:00\n```\n\n---\n\n## Implementation tasks\n\n### 1 — Move `SECRET_KEY_BASE` to `.secrets` (small, high value)\n`SECRET_KEY_BASE` is currently regenerated on every reinstall, invalidating all active sessions. Move it to `data/rails/.secrets` alongside the AR encryption keys so it survives reinstall.\n\n### 2 — Version file\nWrite `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/version` on install with the image tag/digest. Read it in a `sandcastle version` subcommand.\n\n### 3 — `.env` split (`env` + `env.defaults`)\nRestructure installer to write `env.defaults` (all vars with defaults) separately from `env` (operator values). Merge at docker-compose level via multiple `env_file` entries.\n\n### 4 — `sandcastle backup` / `sandcastle restore`\nStandalone backup command. `pg_dump` + secrets snapshot. Auto-invoked at start of upgrade.\n\n### 5 — `sandcastle upgrade`\nThe main missing piece. Pull → backup → migrate → restart → health check → rollback on failure.\n\n### 6 — `sandcastle status`\nShow installed version, service health, sandbox count, last backup.\n\n---\n\n## Out of scope for this issue\n\n- Blue-green zero-downtime deploys (nice to have, complex, probably overkill for self-hosted)\n- Automatic upgrade scheduling / unattended upgrades\n- Multi-node / HA deployments\nEOF\n)\" \\\n  --label \"enhancement\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create comprehensive GitHub issue for install/upgrade lifecycle"
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "gh issue create \\\n  --title \"feat: add solid_errors for self-hosted error tracking\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Overview\n\nAdd [solid_errors](https://github.com/fractaledmind/solid_errors) for database-backed error tracking. Fits naturally alongside Solid Queue / Cache / Cable — same pattern, no external service required.\n\n## What it does\n\nHooks into Rails' built-in error reporting API (`Rails.error.report`). Every unhandled exception is persisted to the database and surfaced in a web UI mounted at `/errors` (admin-only). Errors are grouped by fingerprint, show occurrence count, backtrace, request context, and resolved/unresolved state.\n\n## Implementation\n\n**1. Gemfile**\n```ruby\ngem \"solid_errors\"\n```\n\n**2. Install**\n```bash\nbin/rails solid_errors:install:migrations\nbin/rails db:migrate\n```\n\n**3. Mount in routes (admin-only)**\n```ruby\n# config/routes.rb\nauthenticate :user, ->(u) { u.admin? } do\n  mount SolidErrors::Engine, at: \"/admin/errors\"\nend\n```\n\n**4. Separate database (consistent with Solid* pattern)**\n\nAdd `solid_errors` database to `config/database.yml` alongside the existing `cache`, `queue`, and `cable` databases:\n\n```yaml\nerrors:\n  <<: *default\n  database: sandcastle_production_errors\n  migrations_paths: db/errors_migrate\n```\n\nAdd to `docker/postgres/init-databases.sh` and the PostgreSQL init script in the installer.\n\n**5. HTTP Basic Auth fallback**\n\n`solid_errors` supports HTTP Basic Auth as an alternative to Devise/Rails auth mounting. Since we use Rails 8 session auth, the `authenticate` block above is cleaner.\n\n## Why this over alternatives\n\n- **No external service** — fits the self-hosted philosophy\n- **Already have the DB** — zero new infrastructure\n- **Rails-native** — uses `Rails.error.report`, works with existing exception handling\n- **Admin UI included** — no custom views needed\n- **Same family** as Solid Queue/Cache/Cable already in use\n\n## Related\n\n- #42 (install/upgrade lifecycle) — `sandcastle_production_errors` DB needs to be included in `sandcastle backup`\nEOF\n)\" \\\n  --label \"enhancement\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create GitHub issue for solid_errors"
}
```

> AGENT

There's the bug — `ensure_image` always tries to pull, never checks if the image already exists locally. Fix it:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Pgohz8nuR6QM1tKGxNXZCH
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Public method for job usage\n  def ensure_image(image)\n    Docker::Image.create(\"fromImage\" => image)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    raise Error, \"Failed to pull image #{image}: #{e.message}\"\n  end",
  "new_string": "  # Public method for job usage\n  def ensure_image(image)\n    Docker::Image.get(image)\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    Docker::Image.create(\"fromImage\" => image)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    raise Error, \"Failed to pull image #{image}: #{e.message}\"\n  end"
}
```

> AGENT

Full walkthrough complete. Summary of what happened — and the bug we caught:

---

**Walkthrough results:**

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| `create` `thies-test` | ✅ Running |
| Write marker `/root/marker.txt` | ✅ `hello-from-snapshot` |
| `snapshot` → `sc-snap-thies:v1` | ✅ Created |
| `create` `thies-test-clone` from snapshot | ✅ Marker preserved in clone |
| Overwrite marker → `restore` to `v1` | ✅ `hello-from-snapshot` restored |
| `destroy` both | ✅ Only `quantum-phoenix` remains |

**Bug found and fixed:** `ensure_image` always tried to pull from a remote registry, even for local snapshot images (`sc-snap-thies:*` created by `docker commit`). Fixed by checking `Docker::Image.get` first and only pulling on `NotFoundError`. Also committed:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01GbEGXkc8jhoH9ww6eMjTfs
```json
{
  "command": "git add app/services/sandbox_manager.rb && git commit -m \"fix(sandbox): check local image before pulling in ensure_image\n\nSnapshot images (sc-snap-thies:*) are created locally via docker commit\nand never pushed to a registry. ensure_image was calling\nDocker::Image.create unconditionally which attempts a remote pull,\nfailing with 'pull access denied'. Now checks Docker::Image.get first\nand only pulls if the image is not found locally.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" -- app/services/sandbox_manager.rb && git push",
  "description": "Commit and push ensure_image fix"
}
```
